- **His question, 07:06,** in `briefs/lighthouse_the_room.md`: how to make this seat leaner without losing what makes it itself, when neither of us can tell what a tweak changes.
- **Measured, one run:** a node pass over this session's transcript (`…\C--Consonance-instances-librarian\0c0c0c0b-0000-4000-8000-00000000115b.jsonl`), summing each assistant message's `usage` since 09:45Z, one count per message id.

At about 3.7 bytes a token that is about 38,000 tokens for the whole shell and about 25,000 for the part that carries the self (the seat's rules, BOOT, the cards). The bytes are measured; the token figures are estimates from them.
- **So:** the self is about 5 % of what is re-read on a median call, and the whole shell about 8 %. The other 92 % is working context: tool output, hand-backs read, my own replies.
- **What follows:** making this seat lean does not need a change to BOOT, the cards or the map. The levers are elsewhere: compact sooner (the context grew from 149k to 695k in 3.3 hours); fewer calls (each tool round re-reads everything, and I made three calls for these sizes where one would do); keep bulk out (listings, long sections), by handing reads to subagents that return a path and a conclusion.
- **On his worry, from the record:** a large context did not protect recall tonight. I missed the dead lock holder at 05:52 with about 400k in the window; the seat's own rules record the same on 2026-08-23 (seven files carried, the method in them missed). Maintenance law 3 says crowding shrinks recall.
- **For any change that does touch the shell:** the instrument exists on disk, the continuity test registered on 2026-11-03 (`loop/continuity_test_registration_2026-11-03.md`, question sets and keys in `handback/p-d627-p4b-questions-*`). Run before and after, it says what a tweak changed. Not checked by me: whether it has been run.

- **What it registers:** the body hook adds `session`, `note` and `note_lines` to its ledger row at each wake, for the librarian's cwd only; the gate takes the latest row of the same session and the same note stamped more than 10 s before the current wake, and S is that count plus one; no such row, or a count larger than the note is now, means the whole file. Coverage is the union of the line numbers the Reads returned (P checked that a Read's result carries them, from the offset when there is one); a cut `cat` covers nothing; missing lines are named as ranges; own-append as now. Seven mutants. Limits named: the note is assumed to grow by appending; two wakes within 10 s read as one; a missing row makes S earlier, never later. The build is wrong if the three fixture cases do not come out deny, allow, deny, or if a recorded `note_lines` differs from `wc -l` at that wake.
- **P's question on my fixture's ":543":** line 543 of today's note is the heading of the wake entry itself, written five minutes after the 12:50 wake (checked now: :543 is "## 12:55 — WAKE AFTER THE 12:50 COMPACTION", :542 is blank). P's reading (a count of 543, S 544, missing 544 to 689) is a fixture of the same rule with a different number. Either stands if the fixture states the recorded count; the recorded case is 542.

Whether a runner's forefoot does the same is a reading, not an argument: **the runner's ball (the two metatarsal markers' mid) against the ankle (the malleoli) along the run, through the last tenth of stance, at 6.97 and 9.47 m/s,** with the marker reader that already prints toe, ball and ankle. Then: if the runner's ball goes behind the ankle by a like amount, the row is withdrawn by name with that figure in its comment; if it does not, the over-pitch is a defect of a rigid foot and becomes its own lap (a ball joint), registered then.
- **Until that reading:** the second row stays in the file as a printed measure with its history, not as an assertion, amended by name by this ruling.
